for one finite Schwartz seminorm pN depending on the source types. If the source labels split into two

Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into
one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order
n is required, because each tree edge sees only its two endpoint clusters as distinguished roots. For
bounded roots, every edge has the pointwise connector (117); excess attachments are summed by the
unused strict KP margin, and the deterministic allocation costs at most the displayed factorial. For
renormalized local sources, first contract every contact subtree into its finite local source vertex. The
remaining edges join disjoint clusters and carry the separated-support exponential from Theorem 8.9(b).
The local vertices are bounded by the triangular source-renormalization seminorms. Repeated Schur
summation of the remaining edges, with one Schwartz L1 norm at the tree root, gives (120) and the
separated-group exponential.
Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time

the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This
proves the claim.
Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

Finite source multiplets and integration For every D < ∞, let J≤D be the finite-dimensional
renormalized curvature-jet source multiplet. The contact hierarchy preserves the engineering filtration.
On J≤D , δω is a finite matrix, allowing same-dimension composite-operator mixing.
Lemma 8.44 (Rotation algebra on finite source multiplets). The contact derivations obey

                                               [δω , δη ] = δ[ω,η]

on every J≤D and are compatible under D 7→ D + 2.
Proof. At finite cutoff the contact derivations are obtained by differentiating the geometric pullback of test
profiles, tensor indices and local source words. Geometric Lie derivatives obey the displayed commutator
identity exactly. Finite triangular source renormalization conjugates this finite-dimensional action and
therefore preserves the commutator. Dimension-raised regulator contacts vanish by Lemma 8.41; the
projective limit and the inclusions of source multiplets preserve the identity.
   Every finite-dimensional real representation of so(4) integrates to the simply connected group Spin(4).
The source multiplets consist of bosonic tensors built from curvature and covariant derivatives, so a 2π
rotation acts trivially; equivalently, their geometric tensor action factors through SO(4).
Theorem 8.45 (SO(4) covariance of the Schwinger family). Let ρD be the integrated finite-dimensional
action on J≤D . Then for every R ∈ SO(4),

Exact lattice reflection extends the covariance to O(4).
Proof. Along a smooth path R(s) in SO(4), differentiate the transformed Schwinger function. The
derivative is the left side of (165) with ω = R′ (s)R(s)−1 and therefore vanishes. Lemma 8.44 gives path

finite source normalization.



                                                       88
Osterwalder–Schrader continuation Let Dloc be the local field-polynomial test algebra. The O(4)
covariance proved above is an identity of the Euclidean Schwinger system. Rotations preserving the
chosen time-zero hyperplane act directly on the positive-time quotient; rotations mixing Euclidean time
with space need not preserve that quotient and are not asserted to act unitarily there.
Theorem 8.46 (Euclidean covariance and Poincaré reconstruction). The Schwinger family satisfies the
full O(4) covariance axiom. Together with reflection positivity, permutation symmetry, temperedness
and continuous translations, this gives by the Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction theorem a strongly
continuous unitary representation of the proper orthochronous Poincaré group on the reconstructed Hilbert
space, with translation spectrum in the closed forward cone. Spatial rotations and translations are obtained
directly on the OS quotient; Lorentz boosts arise from analytic continuation of the Euclidean rotation
covariance.
Proof. Theorem 8.45 is precisely the Euclidean covariance identity required for every finite tensor/source
multiplet, and the nested source construction extends it to the complete chosen local field family. The
direct OS theorem, Lemma 8.14, and Lemma 8.13 supply the remaining reconstruction hypotheses,
including the linear-growth condition. The standard Osterwalder–Schrader covariance reconstruction
then produces the positive- energy Poincaré representation. No unitary action of a Euclidean rotation
that changes the reflection hyperplane is assumed on the intermediate OS quotient.

9     Continuum reconstruction and spectral gap
9.1    Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction and clustering gap
The common Schwinger functional Let D be the countable algebra generated by positive-time
translates of the bounded positive-scale cylinders and the normalized finite local source fields constructed
by the marked hierarchy. The multiscale source-tree theorem supplies a projective Schwinger functional
S on D with the following properties:
(S1) temperedness and continuity in every Schwartz test tensor;
(S2) stationarity, gauge invariance, permutation symmetry, and reflection positivity;
(S3) continuous Euclidean translations;
(S4) for every two field polynomials F, G with separated supports,

                              |S(F G) − S(F )S(G)| ≤ CF,G e−m dist1 (supp F,supp G)                   (166)

     with one m > 0 independent of the chosen polynomials;
(S5) the OS linear-growth estimate of Lemma 8.13 on every finite source multiplet.
The constants CF,G may depend on the fixed fields and test functions; the exponent m does not.
Theorem 9.1 (Continuous OS reconstruction). Let Θ be time reflection and D+ the positive-time
subalgebra. The form
                                        ⟨F, G⟩OS = S(ΘF G)
is positive semidefinite. Its quotient and completion give a separable Hilbert space H, vacuum Ω = [1],
and a strongly continuous self-adjoint contraction semigroup

                                         T (t) = e−tH ,    H ≥ 0.

Proof. Reflection positivity gives the positive form and the null ideal N = {F : ∥F ∥OS = 0}. Positive-time
translations preserve D+ for sufficiently small translations on each compactly supported word and define
contractions on the quotient; the usual time-zero limiting argument extends them to all t ≥ 0. For a core
vector [F ],
                                        2
                      ∥T (t)[F ] − [F ]∥ = S(ΘF τ2t F ) − 2ℜS(ΘF τt F ) + S(ΘF F ),
which tends to zero by translation continuity. Contractivity and density give strong continuity on H.
Reflection symmetry makes the semigroup self-adjoint, and the spectral theorem gives T (t) = e−tH with
H ≥ 0.

Spectral consequence of one common clustering rate Let D+,0 be the centered positive-time
core,
                              D+,0 = {F ∈ D+ : S(F ) = 0}.
For F ∈ D+,0 let µF be the positive spectral measure of H in the vector [F ].


                                                     89
Lemma 9.2 (Core spectral support). For every F ∈ D+,0 ,

                                              supp µF ⊂ [m, ∞).                                       (167)

Proof. Let the time support of F lie in a fixed compact interval. For all sufficiently large t, the supports
of ΘF and τt F are separated by at least t − cF . Since S(F ) = 0, (166) and OS reconstruction give
                               Z ∞
                          0≤        e−tλ dµF (λ) = ⟨[F ], T (t)[F ]⟩ ≤ CF e−mt .                       (168)
                                  0

Fix ε > 0. Then
                                      e−(m−ε)t µF ([0, m − ε]) ≤ CF e−mt .
Multiplication by e(m−ε)t and t → ∞ gives µF ([0, m − ε]) = 0. Letting ε ↓ 0 proves (167).

Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies

                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

The vacuum is unique.
Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
   For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

Osterwalder–Schrader continuation Let Dloc be the local field-polynomial test algebra. The O(4)
covariance proved above is an identity of the Euclidean Schwinger system. Rotations preserving the
chosen time-zero hyperplane act directly on the positive-time quotient; rotations mixing Euclidean time
with space need not preserve that quotient and are not asserted to act unitarily there.
Theorem 8.46 (Euclidean covariance and Poincaré reconstruction). The Schwinger family satisfies the
full O(4) covariance axiom. Together with reflection positivity, permutation symmetry, temperedness
and continuous translations, this gives by the Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction theorem a strongly
continuous unitary representation of the proper orthochronous Poincaré group on the reconstructed Hilbert
space, with translation spectrum in the closed forward cone. Spatial rotations and translations are obtained
directly on the OS quotient; Lorentz boosts arise from analytic continuation of the Euclidean rotation
covariance.
Proof. Theorem 8.45 is precisely the Euclidean covariance identity required for every finite tensor/source
multiplet, and the nested source construction extends it to the complete chosen local field family. The
direct OS theorem, Lemma 8.14, and Lemma 8.13 supply the remaining reconstruction hypotheses,
including the linear-growth condition. The standard Osterwalder–Schrader covariance reconstruction
then produces the positive- energy Poincaré representation. No unitary action of a Euclidean rotation
that changes the reflection hyperplane is assumed on the intermediate OS quotient.

9     Continuum reconstruction and spectral gap
9.1    Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction and clustering gap
The common Schwinger functional Let D be the countable algebra generated by positive-time
translates of the bounded positive-scale cylinders and the normalized finite local source fields constructed
by the marked hierarchy. The multiscale source-tree theorem supplies a projective Schwinger functional
S on D with the following properties:
(S1) temperedness and continuity in every Schwartz test tensor;

                              |S(F G) − S(F )S(G)| ≤ CF,G e−m dist1 (supp F,supp G)                   (166)

                                        ⟨F, G⟩OS = S(ΘF G)

vector [F ],

                      ∥T (t)[F ] − [F ]∥ = S(ΘF τ2t F ) − 2ℜS(ΘF τt F ) + S(ΘF F ),
which tends to zero by translation continuity. Contractivity and density give strong continuity on H.
Reflection symmetry makes the semigroup self-adjoint, and the spectral theorem gives T (t) = e−tH with
H ≥ 0.

Spectral consequence of one common clustering rate Let D+,0 be the centered positive-time
core,
                              D+,0 = {F ∈ D+ : S(F ) = 0}.
For F ∈ D+,0 let µF be the positive spectral measure of H in the vector [F ].


                                                     89
Lemma 9.2 (Core spectral support). For every F ∈ D+,0 ,

                                              supp µF ⊂ [m, ∞).                                       (167)

Proof. Let the time support of F lie in a fixed compact interval. For all sufficiently large t, the supports
of ΘF and τt F are separated by at least t − cF . Since S(F ) = 0, (166) and OS reconstruction give
                               Z ∞
                          0≤        e−tλ dµF (λ) = ⟨[F ], T (t)[F ]⟩ ≤ CF e−mt .                       (168)
                                  0

Fix ε > 0. Then
                                      e−(m−ε)t µF ([0, m − ε]) ≤ CF e−mt .
Multiplication by e(m−ε)t and t → ∞ gives µF ([0, m − ε]) = 0. Letting ε ↓ 0 proves (167).

Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies

                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

The vacuum is unique.
Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
   For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

10.2     Uniform endpoint interpolation
Theorem 10.2 (Uniform endpoint interpolation master theorem). Fix the positive-time profile f and a
                                                   √
physical weak scale r, put ur = u(r) and gr = ur , and use g rather than u = g 2 as the interpolation

fixed finite defect root, the following hold on the full segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr :
    (i) the graph-segment identities are C 2 and exact before every limit;
   (ii) the stable/wrapping orbit rows, the pre-owner two-mark compact entrance row, and the terminal
        packet-shell row have constants independent of cutoff, volume, preceding weak depth, crossing cell,
        and terminal depth M ;
  (iii) differentiation is performed inside each fixed crossing cell before ownership, never on the crossing
        index, and the resulting total derivatives extend across cell boundaries;
  (iv) the no-crossing terminal remainder tends to zero through two g-derivatives uniformly as M → ∞.

1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .
Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #
                             G,ρ
                                                       X                Y
                                        −1
                          dµΛ,u (U ) = ZΛ,u exp −u  −1
                                                           wG,ρ (Up )     dmG (Ue )
                                                         p             e

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .


                                                     3
Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime sy

the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,


                                                     5
where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

=== reflection-positive hit 1 at 805 ===
 Yang–Mills Existence, Mass Gap, and Nontriviality
for Fixed Compact Simple Gauge Groups via Karcher
      Blocking and Exact Haar–Pivot Matching
                                             Harold D. Kirk III1,2,∗
                         1
                             University of Memphis, Memphis, Tennessee 38152, USA
                                        2
                                            ORCID: 0009-0000-6014-8451

                                                August 3, 2026


                                                     Abstract
        Let G be a fixed compact connected simple Lie group and let ρ be a fixed faithful finite-dimensional
    unitary representation. We construct four-dimensional pure G Yang–Mills theory as a zero-spacing,
    infinite-volume limit of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures. The Wilson plaquette function
    is normalized at the identity to the background quadratic form used to define the coupling u = g 2 .
    A weighted forty-nine-path Karcher block, complete sixteen-alias normal section, charged chart-
    complement partition, and permanent rooted domain produce a uniform scale-two map on a centered
    invariant graph,
                                                 11 CA (G) log 2
                               u(2L) = u(L) +                    u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ).
                                                     24π 2

    The complete linear block certificate is independent of color and tensors with Ig ; all nonlinear
    constants are finite and may depend on G. At the physical handoff, a group-dependent product
    Sobolev order 2sG > dG /2 gives a Peter–Weyl Fourier–Wiener landing for the exact one-pivot and
    multipivot densities. Separated Haar pivots contract nonvacuum compact flux, optical covariance
    contracts normal-field stretches, and all remaining vertices retain their support charge. A single
    marked replica–BKAR forest then gives a boundary-to-center two-root connector and one physical
    clustering rate. General invariant-jet transport, displacement and stress Ward identities, finite
    dimension-four mixing, and a dimension-six rotation regulator yield a tempered, reflection-positive,
    translation-continuous, O(4)-covariant Schwinger family. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction gives a
    positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with unique vacuum and a strictly positive Hamiltonian
    gap. Endpoint-safe curvature-source transport produces a nonzero local vector with positive spectral
    mass in a finite interval above the gap. The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet
    observable theory. No estimate is asserted uniformly over the class of compact simple groups.

1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .
Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #
                             G,ρ
                                                       X                Y
                                        −1
                          dµΛ,u (U ) = ZΛ,u exp −u  −1
                                                           wG,ρ (Up )     dmG (Ue )
                                                         p             e

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .


                                                     3
Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.
1.4     External inputs and exact proof boundary
Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In

The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .
Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .

Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squa

Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that

exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].

the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and

acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber

 types. If the source labels split into two
groups with mutually separated supports, the connected cumulant has the additional common factor e−md .
Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into
one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order
n is required, because each tree edge sees only its two endpoint clusters as distinguished roots. For
bounded roots, every edge has the pointwise connector (117); excess attachments are summed by the
unused strict KP margin, and the deterministic allocation costs at most the displayed factorial. For
renormalized local sources, first contract every contact subtree into its finite local source vertex. The
remaining edges join disjoint clusters and carry the separated-support exponential from Theorem 8.9(b).
The local vertices are bounded by the triangular source-renormalization seminorms. Repeated Schur
summation of the remaining edges, with one Schwartz L1 norm at the tree root, gives (120) and the
separated-group exponential.
Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
                                               11N log 2
                             u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
                                                 24π 2
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

                                             (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)

nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.

Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete

quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).

Compatibility of the physical scale with the common diagonal. Let f be the profile of

Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields
Yr (f ), ΘYr (f ), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger
limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is
not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0
along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves
countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the
same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform
finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
                                               11N log 2
                             u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
                                                 24π 2
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

A     Endpoint packet-shell verification
This appendix supplies the detailed proof of Theorem 10.4. It is part of the same manuscript and
uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint
theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance,
and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem.

Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ,

                                                      X

For every 0 < a′ < a0 , the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same

   If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the
canonical relative factor
                                             (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)

Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color,
translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg
coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated
after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0.
Reflection gives Tλ (−p) = Tλ (p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor
formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum
derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ .
   The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension
four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this
observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
   The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.

Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete
normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the
permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row

                                                 η0 (g) ≤ Cg,

while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row

                                              η1 (g) + η2 (g) ≤ C.

The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero
                                             2
at g = 0 and are bounded by Cm g −pm e−c/g for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g 2 ) is
made.
Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the
quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect

atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

for one finite Schwartz seminorm pN depending on the source types. If the source labels split into two

Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into
one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order

separated-group exponential.
Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.
